# The test-suite audit of 2026-09-25: register

Every finding is a contradiction pair quoted by content from the repository at trunk `485a7d9` (the read-only worktree `../n-Op-audit-baseline`). A finding enters this register only if its quote re-grounds at that commit when this page is built and a blind verifier confirmed it (round 2 or round 3); every S1 was also reproduced by a second route and re-checked by the conductor, and every contested S2 was adjudicated by the conductor. Severities: S1 wrong result, S2 wrong statement, S3 silent deviation (built or measured differently from a pre-code document with no reason recorded where readers look), S4 latent defect, S5 cosmetic. Nothing here quotes a pseudopotential title or a volumetric array.

## Calibration and coverage

**Blind key.** provenance/blind-key.md (52 items, sha256 335f7c25...003d, fixed 2026-09-25 09:10 before any auditor started). Of 45 blind items the auditors found 29 through findings and 2 more through the commitment ledger (64% and 69%; 70% excluding the two items the pre-check had already refuted). Memory-file items: 4 of 5; hinted items: 2 of 2 (the memory index was not injected into workflow agents (transcripts checked 2026-09-25), so the hinted class is hinted in name only).

**Overlap estimate.** Lincoln-Petersen capture-recapture on the 14 files where the three overlap auditors reported at least one finding (of the 16 they re-read): owners found 126, the overlap auditors 46, both 21; estimated total 276 (Chapman 270), so owner recall about 46% and union recall about 55%. heterogeneous detectability biases the estimate upward (overlap auditors reported many S4/S5 items the owners did not look for), and the two overlap files with no overlap findings are excluded; read it as a lower bound on recall, beside the blind key's 64-69%.

## The commitment ledger

1090 commitments extracted from the pre-code documents (test-suite.md at 85d0be5, the specs at 9f0b34a, the manifests and README at 155112c); 1090 resolved against 485a7d9: 225 deviation, 713 match, 133 not_built, 19 unclear. Of the deviations: 16 commit_or_comment_only, 108 recorded, 101 silent; 15 recorded only after the affected result existed. The full ledger is `audits/2026-09-25/findings/round1b-ledger-rows-final.jsonl` with `round1b-resolutions-merged.jsonl`.

## What was not checked, and deviations from the plan

- **Agents on Fable.** The plan said every agent runs on Opus. Round 1b's relaunch set no model, so the auditors G, O1, O2, O3, the ledger extractions L4-L7 and the resolver batches L1:1-4, L3:1-5, L2:1, L2:3, L6:1 and L6:3 ran on Fable 5.1 (the conductor's model). Their results stand and were verified like everything else; every later agent (round 2, the last resolver, round 3) ran on Opus.
- **The conductor's S2 re-check is a sample.** The plan committed the conductor to re-checking every S1 and S2. Every S1 (26) and every contested S2 was re-checked; of the 303 S2 that round 2 confirmed, a seeded sample of 48 (seed 20261009) was re-checked in the conductor's own context, 47 confirmed and 1 reproduced by the verifier only, none refuted. The other 255 stand on the blind verifier plus the mechanical grounding.
- **The evidence-command replay** of every S1-S3 finding, a mechanical-gate step, was skipped: the round-2 verifiers re-derived evidence by their own routes instead.
- **S4 and S5 verification.** S4 findings carry one blind verifier; S5 findings are grounded mechanically and not verified, by the round-2 design.
- **Round 3** re-read the 56 files round 1 covered only superficially; its findings were grounded, deduplicated against the 618 and verified before entering this register (see the round-3 section). Coverage after round 3 is complete at the file level; depth remains what one or two readers give a file.
- **The salvaged auditors.** Three round-1 auditors (structure-to-density, convolutional, results page) were stopped by the session limit after saving complete files; their saved files stand as their returns, calibrated against the 19 auditors whose saved files matched their returns.
- **The unparsable resolver file.** Resolver batch L6:1's saved file could not be parsed; its 40 rows were re-resolved by one Opus agent on 9 October.
- **Store integrity.** The guards (store, census, training and figure caches read-only; pseudopotential files unreadable) were on from the audit's first gate on 25 September until the end of Phase D on 9 October and restored from the recorded modes (19,202 targets, 0 failures). The fingerprint of `/Pool/VASP_DATA/_derived` and `_census` taken after the restore matches the one taken after the first gate in every one of its 10,808 entries (content, time and permission bits), so nothing the audit or the flagship inspection ran wrote to the store.
- **Not in scope:** 1-archive/, the pnp-op seed paper, the ladder worktree's unmerged branch (except where trunk quotes it), and /Pool data the test-suite work never read. The fixed-point rung's evaluation (uncommitted in the ladder worktree) is not judged here.

## S1: wrong result (28)

#### D1-raw-data-001 (S1, wrong result) in `operators/data/store.py`

- **Quote:** # every volumetric file repeats the same geometry, so the first one read wins
- **Counter (command, `/home/javier/Projects/Physics/Programs/n-Op-audit-scratch/D1-raw-data/work/aeccar_evidence.py`):** aeccar_evidence.py on diamond/triads_defects/Li-B-P/c8-c53-c1/config2/GGA-PBE (archive defect_set/8c7513908f838bdd): AECCAR0 and AECCAR1 header positions sit 0.0000 A from POSCAR and 1.0934 A from CONTCAR, while CHGCAR, AECCAR2, ELFCAR and LOCPOT sit 0.0000 A from CONTCAR. The grid values follow the headers: Li atom moved 1.093 A -> AECCAR0 3.426e+04 at the initial nucleus vs 1.072 at the final one, AECCAR1 588.8 vs 59.1, AECCAR2 177.6 vs 1179; P atom moved 0.570 A -> AECCAR0 5.861e+06 vs 97.4, AECCAR1 3.579e+04 vs 263. The archive's positions are 0.0000 A from CONTCAR and 1.0934 A from POSCAR. Store-wide (disp_counts.py): the AECCAR1 header geometry differs from the CHGCAR geometry by >0.001 A in 173/182 alloy, 120/196 defect and 90/170 supercell runs, and by >0.1 A in 130 alloy and 68 defect runs.
- **Claim:** VASP writes AECCAR0 and AECCAR1 on the starting (POSCAR) geometry and CHGCAR, AECCAR2, ELFCAR and LOCPOT on the final one, so in every relaxation run the archive's single lattice/positions (taken from CHGCAR, the first file read) do not describe its all_electron_core_density and superposed_atomic_density fields. Nothing in the sidecar records this, and the floors that read superposed_atomic_density (floors.Superposed_Atomic_Density_Errors, deep_dft/floors.py) treat it as the superposition of the stored structure. For the one run without CHGCAR (supercell shear_xy_g0.010) the archive geometry itself comes from AECCAR0, i.e. the starting geometry, beside a final-geometry localization field.
- **Fix proposed:** Record the AECCAR0/AECCAR1 header geometry beside those two fields (for example superposition_positions) and flag in the sidecar every run whose AECCAR0/1 geometry differs from CHGCAR's; score superposition floors only where the superposition sits on the density's geometry (static runs, or the chained static partner's AECCAR1), and replace the comment with 'AECCAR0 and AECCAR1 carry the starting geometry; the others carry the final one'.
- **Status:** confirmed; reproduced by a second route: yes; conductor re-check: confirmed; fix: 4ea1b1a (F12)

#### D1-raw-data-002 (S1, wrong result) in `operators/stage0-report.md`

- **Quote:** - alloy_ensemble: median 27.02%, interquartile 3.64%, runs 182

- **Claim:** The alloy superposed-atomic-density floor scores AECCAR1 built on the unrelaxed geometry against the relaxed density in 173 of 182 runs, so the reported median and spread include relaxation displacement rather than only the missing bonding; wherever the corpus holds the superposition on the relaxed geometry, the error is lower by up to 3.45 points. The line overstates the floor that structure-to-field members and the alloy transfer axis are scored against.

#### M10-structure-to-density-001 (S1, wrong result) in `operators/deep_dft/results.json`

- **Quote:** "interquartile": 11.583332
- **Counter (command, `/home/javier/Projects/Physics/Programs/n-Op-audit-scratch/M10-structure-to-density/salted_fit_eval.py`):** Recomputing the floor with the member's own ridge, sampler and seeds (shell features from a vectorized copy of floors.Species_Shell_Features, calibrated to 2e-16 relative against the original) reproduces the row: median 0.111713, interquartile 11.583337, interval [2.832036, 7.337819]. Per-unit medians: the 12 units whose elements all occur in folds 1-4 score 0.0931-0.1127; the 9 held-out-chemistry units (dopants Cu, Rh, Bi, Tl, Fr, Sc, Ba, I, Al, each with 0 training runs) score 11.41-12.01. On every held-out run the ridge correction averages +8.40 e/A^3 over the cell (cell-mean density 0.70 e/A^3). Mechanism (salted_mechanism.py): the fitted raw intercept is 537.78 e/A^3 = 64 x 8.40, cancelled on 64-atom training structures by per-atom shell terms (each element's 3.0 A shell coefficient times 1/V is about -13.3; carbon -13.24); Species_Shell_Features drops an atom whose element is not in the vocabulary (element_column_of.get(symbol, -1)), so one atom's share stays uncancelled. Controls: scoring the unseen atom as a carbon column gives NMAE 0.10-0.24 on those runs (SAD alone 0.15-0.32); raising the ridge penalty from the default 1e-6 to 0.01 gives 0.259 on run a9d5ea28a3138aa9 (11.67 as built). With the offset removed (salted_alternatives.py) the same floor has interquartile 0.009-0.029 and interval [0.104-0.111, 0.123-0.136]; the median only moves to 0.108-0.111.
- **Claim:** The reduced-salted floor row's interquartile (11.583332) and 95% mean interval ([2.832035, 7.337816]) are not a spread of ridge corrections but the product of a construction defect: every fold-zero run carrying an element absent from the training folds gets a near-uniform +8.40 e/A^3 offset (about twelve times the mean density), so all 9 held-out-chemistry units score 11.4-12.0 normalized MAE while the other 12 score 0.09-0.11.
- **Fix proposed:** Remove the common-mode blow-up before re-measuring: use a real ridge penalty on the standardized design (1e-6 against a Gram diagonal of order 1e5 is ordinary least squares), and/or give atoms of elements outside the vocabulary a fallback (e.g. host or pooled) shell column instead of silently dropping them; then regenerate the row. Expected: interquartile about 0.01-0.03, interval about [0.10, 0.14], median 0.108-0.111, gate b bar 0.036-0.037 instead of 0.037238.
- **Status:** confirmed; reproduced by a second route: yes; defense: recorded (after the result); conductor re-check: confirmed
- **Adjudication:** the report flags the spread but attributes it to ridge ill-conditioning on 'a handful' of units; the cause is a construction defect (a near-uniform +8.4 e/A^3 offset on every unit whose element is absent from training, 9 of 21), so the recording is partial and wrong about the mechanism

#### M10-structure-to-density-003 (S1, wrong result) in `operators/deep_dft/IMPLEMENTATION.md`

- **Quote:** carries hydrogen atoms in its geometry but only `Si` and `C` titles in its OUTCAR (a stale
- **Counter (command, `/Pool/VASP_DATA/_census/runs.jsonl`):** Census rows for the two runs whose dopant the member keys as H: diamond/single_defect/IVA-element-single-impurity/Si/GGA-PBE and .../Si/HSE06 have POSCAR species line ['H', 'C'] with counts [1, 63], pseudopotentials Si then C (element symbols of the titles, in order), and NELECT 256.0000; the store's electron_count and the charge-density integral are 256.0 too. VASP assigns the POTCAR blocks to the POSCAR ion types by position (VASP Wiki, POTCAR: for several species the potentials are concatenated in the order the species appear in POSCAR), so the single type-1 atom was computed with the Si potential: 1 x 4 + 63 x 4 = 256 valence electrons, whereas a hydrogen atom would give 1 + 252 = 253. The store's species array still says H, the member routes it to the unknown row, and both floors score it as hydrogen: re-scoring with the Si label changes the reduced-salted values of 34c5ebd36d43713f / bd39ceefa779d83c from 0.1016 / 0.1058 to 0.1004 / 0.1016 and the nearest-copy values from 0.0298 / 0.0326 to 0.0651 / 0.0666 (silicon_relabel.py); the pooled medians do not move.
- **Claim:** The lone dopant atom of both IVA-element-single-impurity/Si runs (fold zero) is a silicon atom in the calculation, but the species handling labels it hydrogen: the embedding sends it to the unknown row and the reduced-salted and nearest-copy floors score it with hydrogen shells and a hydrogen composition. Matching titles to the species column by name, 'not by position', is exactly what VASP does not do.
- **Fix proposed:** Key each ion type by the title at the same position (as VASP assigns them), or at least relabel an atom from its positional title when the species-line name has no matching title; relabel these two runs Si, re-score the two floors for that unit, and correct the spec sentence (it is a stale POSCAR species line, not hydrogen atoms).
- **Status:** confirmed; reproduced by a second route: yes; conductor re-check: confirmed

The dropped floors are the global affine (0.010466), the two closed-form ridges (0.000005 and 0.000049) and the rank-32 ceiling (0.000002). The report calls the ridges and ceiling 'the informative comparison'; they beat the member (0.002079) by 42x to 1040x, and operator-results.md records the member as '406x worse than the closed-form ridge'. The committed picture instead shows the member far below the only floor it draws.
- **Fix proposed:** Key by the floor's own name: floor_medians = {comparison.group_name: comparison.member_median for comparison in floor_comparisons}. Keep floor_medians['identity'] = member_comparison.floor_median and re-render floors.png. The four context floors then take required improvement 0.0 and identity takes 0.5, as lines 367-368 already intend. (clear-cut)
- **Status:** confirmed; reproduced by a second route: yes; conductor re-check: confirmed

#### R3-M10-deep-dft-001 (S1, wrong result) in `operators/deep_dft/species.py`

- **Quote:** (symbol, title_by_element[symbol]) if symbol in title_by_element else UNKNOWN_SPECIES_KEY
- **Counter (command, ``):** The two fold-zero runs diamond/single_defect/IVA-element-single-impurity/Si/{GGA-PBE,HSE06} (34c5ebd36d43713f, bd39ceefa779d83c) store species C x63 + H x1, but their titles name Si and C, and the census gives o_nelect 256.0000 (the store's electron_count is 256.0 and the density integrates to 256.0). 63 carbons x 4 valence electrons + 4 = 256 means the impurity was computed with the Si pseudopotential; an H impurity would give 63x4 + 1 = 253. VASP takes each ion's potential from POTCAR order, so the 'H' in the POSCAR species line is a stale label. Because the loader matches titles to that stale label, the Si impurity is keyed (unknown, unknown), even though Si is in the training vocabulary (six training runs carry Si). The floors read the same stale species_symbols: Nearest_Copy_Errors copies the H-impurity training run 123f0568753bb34e (diamond/single_defect/IA-element-single-impurity/H/gga-pbe) for both Si runs, and the reduced-salted ridge puts H shells on a Si site.
- **Claim:** The species key for the Si-impurity fold-zero unit is wrong. The loader trusts the stale H label in the species column over the title order that VASP actually used. The nearest-copy and reduced-salted floor values for that unit (1 of 21 fold-zero units) are therefore computed on the wrong chemistry, and the member would embed a Si atom through the untrained unknown row.
- **Fix proposed:** When a run's species set disagrees with the element set its titles name, resolve species by POSCAR/POTCAR position (cross-checked against NELECT), or correct the label in the store. Then rerun the floors for that unit.
- **Status:** confirmed (round 3); reproduced by a second route: yes; defense: recorded
- **Adjudication:** round 3: the species key for the silicon single-impurity unit follows the stale H label (K01, review item R8); the spec records the choice as deliberate but treats the stale label as the chemistry; reproduced by a second route

#### R3-M2-parametric-001 (S1, wrong result) in `operators/factorized_fourier/parametric.py`

- **Quote:** _, value = point.removeprefix("shear_").split("_g")
- **Counter (command, `operators/data/orbits.py`):** Strain_Tensor_Of keeps the plane ('pair, value = point.removeprefix("shear_").split("_g")' then shear[pair] = float(value)); Strain_Level throws it away (and likewise the plane pair for two-angle shear via '_, remainder = ...split("_", 1)'). Measured with arms.py: every one_angle_shear level holds 6 runs (shear_xy, shear_xz, shear_yz x PBE/HSE06) and every two_angle_shear level 6 runs (xy_xz, xy_yz, xz_yz pairs); 'levels mixing points' = 40/40 and 76/76. mix.py: shear_xy vs shear_xz at g=0.05 differ by 3.9% relative L2, and the 6-run level mean differs from each real run by 2.3% (g=0.05) to 4.5% (g=0.1). perrun_all.py: scored per run (same plane, same functional for level and corners) the bracketing floor median is one_angle_shear 9.87e-06 and two_angle_shear 2.53e-04, against the reported 0.000005 and 0.000194 (report.md table rows bracketing_interpolation_floor__one_angle_shear / __two_angle_shear relative_l2); other families agree to 3 digits.
- **Claim:** Strain_Level drops the shear plane, so each one-angle and two-angle shear 'level' pools three differently oriented shear planes; Cached_Level_Field then averages their densities, and the published per-family bracketing floors for these two families are scored against a plane-averaged field no DFT run has, understating the per-run floor by about 2x (one-angle) and 30% (two-angle).
- **Fix proposed:** Key shear levels by (plane or plane pair, values) — e.g. make each plane its own arm, or include the plane in the level — and re-score the floors per run; restate the one-angle and two-angle shear floor medians.
- **Status:** confirmed (round 3); reproduced by a second route: yes

#### S1-metrics-intervals-pooling-001 (S1, wrong result) in `operators/metrics/__init__.py`

- **Quote:** relative L2 error of the below-cutoff and above-cutoff mode bands
- **Counter (command, `operators/metrics/__init__.py`):** Band_Error sums np.linalg.norm over the np.fft.rfftn half-spectrum without doubling the interior last-axis modes (each stands for itself and its unstored conjugate), so modes on the kz=0 and Nyquist planes count double. Output, 8^3 truth cos(2πx)+cos(2πz): an error of 0.1cos(2πz) gives 0.0577 and the same error along x gives 0.0816, while the band's true relative L2 is 0.1/√2 = 0.0707. With cutoff 1e9 (the whole field is the 'low' band) it returns 0.27616 where Relative_L2 of the same pair is 0.27768. On the perovskite fold-0 nearest-angle copy floor (cutoff 8) the low-band median is 0.078624 as coded against 0.085226 full-spectrum, with the worst run off by 16.3%; on the ridge floor 0.039214 against 0.040418, per-run ratio 0.887 to 1.075.
- **Claim:** Frequency_Split_Relative_L2 does not return the relative L2 of each band. Modes on the last axis's kz=0 and Nyquist planes weigh twice as much as every other mode, so the value depends on which axis the error lies along, and it does not reproduce Relative_L2 when the band is the whole field. Every frequency_split_relative_l2_low/high row in operators/deep_operator_network/report.md was computed this way (for example the principal_component fold-0 member at 0.016584 / 0.122894). On real perovskite floor predictions the per-run bias reaches 11-16%.
- **Fix proposed:** Weight half-spectrum entries by 2 except on the kz=0 plane and, for even n, the Nyquist plane (or use np.fft.fftn with the full-grid radius). Add a test that an infinite cutoff reproduces Relative_L2, then regenerate the DeepONet frequency-split rows.
- **Status:** confirmed; reproduced by a second route: yes; conductor re-check: confirmed

#### S1-metrics-intervals-pooling-002 (S1, wrong result) in `operators/data/splits.py`

- **Quote:** one unit per perovskite run, with the duplicated center removed
- **Counter (command, `operators/evaluation/scoring.py`):** Every one of the 25 fold-0 angle-stratum runs has a run in folds 1-4 whose angle triple is a permutation of its own. After the matching grid-axis transpose, the two charge densities differ by at most 8.30e-06 relative L2 (for example alpha 0.8 beta 0.9 gamma 1, fold 4, against alpha 0.9 beta 0.8 gamma 1, fold 3: 3.4e-07). The 25 fold-0 'units' are 19 permutation orbits. The angle stratum's 125 runs form 35 orbits and the length stratum's 124 runs form 34, and every fold-0 length run also has an orbit partner in folds 1-4. The length pair a0.8 b0.9 c1 (fold 2, grid 56x60x64) against a0.9 b0.8 c1 (fold 1, grid 60x56x64) differs by 3.1e-07. scoring.py's Unit_Medians promises 'each exchangeable unit's own median, so symmetry copies inside one orbit count once'.
- **Claim:** The perovskite split makes every run its own exchangeable unit. The 5-atom cell (Nb at the origin, Ag at the body center, O at the three half-edge sites) is symmetric under axis permutation, so runs whose lattice-factor triples are permutations of each other are exact rotated copies of one field. The split scatters these copies across folds, so every fold-0 evaluation run has an exact copy in the training folds, which contradicts the plan's orbit rule in 9.2 ('any random split is invalid by construction'). This was checked field by field for all 25 fold-0 angle runs; for the length stratum it follows from orbit membership, with sample pairs checked field by field. The unit counts are overstated too: 25 'units' are 19 orbits. The perovskite interpolation rows (DeepONet II.1b 'pass/pass', the Galerkin copy and interpolation floors) and their unit counts rest on this split.
- **Fix proposed:** Key perovskite units by stratum plus the sorted factor triple (the permutation orbit), re-assign folds by orbit, and regenerate the perovskite rows, unit counts and verdicts.
- **Status:** confirmed; reproduced by a second route: yes; conductor re-check: confirmed

| N1-substrate-and-framework-001 | `operators/framework/invariance.py` | The comment says no operator on coarse input can beat the truncation null. That is false: the truth's content above the coarse band can be a function of the coarse input, and a pointwise nonlinear map recovers it exactly (skill 1.0). The null is a reference level, not a floor. The axis reports a skill because the model can beat it. The same unbeatable-floor claim is repeated in test_invariance.py ('an operator handed only the coarse input cannot beat carrying the coarse truth back up'; '# the null is the floor of the axis') and in test-suite.md section 9.5(a) ('the irreducible truncation floor'). | confirmed; conductor re-check: confirmed |

| N1-substrate-and-framework-008 | `operators/README.md` | Rule 4 still says arrays are an opaque alias and that the array and autodiff substrate is specified in the implementation documents, not in the README. | N1-substrate-and-framework-010 | `operators/framework/operator.py` | The module docstring says NeuralOperator is a template that chains encoder, composition and readout. Its __call__ body is 'raise NotImplementedError', so it chains nothing. Every working assembly writes its own __call__: 8 packages, and gaussian_plane_wave raises in __init__. Because the base supplies a concrete __call__, an assembly that forgets its own instantiates and fails only when called. A subclass without __call__ constructs fine and raises NotImplementedError on call, which drops the abstract-member protection the README credits to the Protocol base. | confirmed |

| N4-encoders-readouts-wrappers-002 | `operators/residual_correction/IMPLEMENTATION.md` | The spec says a test orbit counts as covered only if every one of its runs is inside the band. report.py:402 does something else: it scores each orbit as the median of its runs' covered indicators (Median_Per_Unit) and then averages over orbits. With 22 test orbits (results.json unit_count 22), each orbit scores 0, 0.5 or 1. The only way to reach 0.977 is 21.5/22 (43/44 = 0.97727), meaning 21 orbits at 1 and one orbit at 0.5, where half its runs fall outside the band. Under the spec's every-run rule that orbit counts as 0, so the coverage could be at most 21/22 = 0.955. The spec sentence and the median code landed together in commit 399dd53, whose message says the spec 'records the built specification'. | confirmed; conductor re-check: confirmed |
| N4-encoders-readouts-wrappers-005 | `operators/wrappers/__init__.py` | Conditioned.__init__ draws condition_scale_weights and condition_shift_weights from normal(0, 1/sqrt(condition_width)), and has done so since it was introduced (72e79da). Experiment: untrained scale weights [[-1.229, -0.945]] and shift weights [[-0.962, -0.249]] change a unit-range field by up to 1.59. Commit 5c6a7e0 repeats the claim ('so untrained weights still leave the channels alone'). | confirmed |

| S1-metrics-intervals-pooling-014 | `operators/factorized_fourier/report.md` | Stage zero fits its climatology and its ridge on the first 80 defect-campaign training runs and takes a flat np.median over the 42 held-out runs. It then takes a unit-level median (Summarize over the defect rows, 21 units). The 57.26% vs 60.28% and 56.20% vs 55.16% gaps therefore mix three changes: training population, pooling level and spin treatment. The sentence assigns the whole gap to spin treatment. | confirmed |
| S1-metrics-intervals-pooling-015 | `operators/deep_dft/report.md` | Only the interquartile range is about 100 times the median (103.7×). The '95% interval' runs from 25× to 66× the median. | S1-metrics-intervals-pooling-016 | `operators/residual_correction/report.md` | The guarantee bounds expected coverage over calibration and test draws. It is not a band that one 22-orbit realization must land in. A realized 0.977 sits 0.036 above the upper end of the expected coverage, less than the module's own 0.063 coverage deviation, and an exactly calibrated band produces a result like this 34-63% of the time. 'Over-covers' and 'the calibrated offset is conservative on this test split' are therefore unsupported, and so is operator-results.md's 'Valid and conservative: it over-covers by 0.036'. | confirmed in part — the guarantee band bounds expected coverage, not one 22-orbit realization; the report's 'over-covers' reading treats it as a band the realization must land in; conformal cluster |

On the committed data both pass by a wide margin (ratio 0.187, median ΔR² 0.9645), so 'equivalently' is false in general but does not flip this verdict. | confirmed |

On those cells the 'equivariance error' mixes model error with physics that is not symmetric under the operation. The members that augment training data with Apply_Grid_Operation rely on the same shape-only assumption. | confirmed; fix: b5c8409 (F3) in part: the helper takes the cells per axis; the cubic-cell gate remains open |

For the campaigns it exists for, Block_Gap_Null reports a 100%+ 'gap' between identical physics. SupercellTwin.tiles: tuple[int, int, int] cannot carry the needed supercell matrix, so section 9.5(b)'s 'exact Fourier retiling' is not what is computed. | confirmed, narrowed — SupercellTwin.tiles (per-axis integers) cannot express the 2-atom primitive to 64-atom cubic relation (a non-diagonal matrix of determinant 32), so Block_Gap_Null as built cannot retile those twins; the README's 'twin shear grid' promise is unmet for them; review beside F3 |

So the band that Coverage_Guarantee reports, and that the calibrator exposes as coverage_guarantee_low/high, holds for one event only: a new unit's median run lands inside the band. When a unit holds several runs, neither the marginal coverage of a run (the usual meaning of marginal coverage) nor the coverage of a whole unit is guaranteed. The only coverage tests (test_parts Test_The_Calibrated_Interval_Covers_Held_Out_Units_At_Its_Level and test_residual_correction's point-prediction test) use Lone_Orbits, one run per unit, where the median changes nothing. At the 23 units IV.1 actually calibrated on, its spread is 0.0553, and the function reports 0.0626, 13% too high. The value is exposed as Inspect()['coverage_deviation'] and used in 3-spread test bands; no report prints it yet. | confirmed |

Signature_Refusals compares signatures within (task, split, block, group), so under mixed conventions two members that scored the same units under one group can be refused, and two that scored different units can be accepted. | confirmed |
| S1-metrics-intervals-pooling-034 | `operators/tests/test_evaluation.py` | The test cannot detect a wrong bar, a wrong second number, or a headline number matched to the wrong metric or group, so its docstring overstates what it verifies. | confirmed in part — blind-key K28: the results-page test matches a headline against any row's median whatever its metric or group; F9 fixed the inverted guard and the skipped members, the any-row matching remains; clear-cut test candidate F19 |
| S1-metrics-intervals-pooling-035 | `operators/metrics/__init__.py` | When the window is longer than an axis, the offsets (np.arange(window) - window // 2) % extent repeat, and 'kernel[offsets] = 1.0 / window' overwrites rather than accumulates. The kernel then sums to less than one, and Structural_Similarity_3d (window 7) would be wrong on any grid axis shorter than 7. No current caller does this, since the smallest structural-similarity grid is 40³. | confirmed |
| S1-metrics-intervals-pooling-036 | `operators/metrics/__init__.py` | Unit keys and values of different lengths are silently truncated. Every unit median and the conformal calibration (ConformalCalibrator.Calibrate) pass through this helper, and elsewhere the package uses zip(..., strict=True). | confirmed |
| S2-floors-and-verdicts-023 | `operators/evaluation/cross_member.py` | Signature_Refusals only compares rows whose (task, split, block, group) all coincide, and Task_Table prints no block column, so two members scored on different blocks or different unit sets are pooled into one task table whenever their group names or block names differ. The module docstring and Test_A_Signature_Mismatch_Refuses ('two members scored on different unit sets for the same task are refused, never averaged together') promise the opposite; the test passes only because both synthetic rows use group 'all'. | confirmed |
| S2-floors-and-verdicts-024 | `operators/evaluation/cross_member.py` | Every_Member_Results skips a canon member without a results.json silently, so the committed cross-member table omits the two members with the most judged numbers (the flagship and the branch-trunk family) without saying so; a reader of cross_member.md sees no member result at all on charge_to_localization. | confirmed |
| S2-floors-and-verdicts-025 | `operators/evaluation/results.py` | Rounding to six decimal places, not significant figures, leaves the smallest floors in results.json with one significant digit, so the artifact the results-page test calls the source cannot reproduce the page's '406x' (it gives 416x), and cross_member.md prints 0.000002 and 0.000005. | confirmed |
| S2-floors-and-verdicts-027 | `operators/tests/test_evaluation.py` | Compare_To_Floor applies `improvement >= required_improvement` to every bar, and the test pins that sitting on the margin passes; the canon's II.2 (kill at <= 5%), I.1 ('kill unless < 0.5x') and II.4 ('kill unless < 0.7x') bars are strict, so a member exactly on those margins would be passed where the canon kills (MIONet's own report pre-registers 'kill if the potential branch adds five percent or less'). | confirmed |
| S2-floors-and-verdicts-028 | `operators/tests/test_floors.py` | The test pins run-level medians, not the orbit-aggregated floors the member is judged against, so a regression in the numbers the verdicts use would pass it. | confirmed |
| S2-floors-and-verdicts-030 | `operators/evaluation/localization_block.py` | The functional labeler tests the whole path for 'hse' (the 'hse06' test is redundant), not the run's own directory, and is exported from operators.evaluation's root; reused on any campaign whose PBE runs sit under an HSE-named parent it mislabels the functional. | confirmed |
| T-training-009 | `operators/training/staged.py` | The resumed path trusts any probe manifest it finds. Nothing records whether the probe passed, so a relaunch after a failed probe skips the probe and trains all three stages from that probe's checkpoint parameters. Two cases: the process died between the failed peak-rate probe and the retry (the manifest then holds the rejected peak rate), or both probes failed and raised RuntimeError (the manifest then holds 0.3 x peak). | confirmed |
| T-training-010 | `operators/training/staged.py` | Sane_Loss_Curve is called only on the probe. There validation_curve[-1] < 10.0 * validation_curve[0] + 1.0 compares one score with itself, so the validation half of the runaway check can only fail on a non-finite value. The docstring's 'neither run away' never applies to the validation score. | confirmed |
| T-training-011 | `operators/training/staged.py` | The absolute +1.0 slack dominates whenever the loss starts well below 1. The divergence probe then passes runs whose loss grew by orders of magnitude, so for the residual-correction member it cannot detect a blow-up that ends below 1.0. | confirmed |
| T-training-012 | `operators/training/staged.py` | An early-stopped final stage has finished, but a relaunch does not skip it. Train resumes from the checkpoint's live parameters, not the best ones, trains at least validation_interval more steps, and then rewrites the checkpoint, curves and manifest (completed_steps, resumed_from_step). If the extra pass sets a new best, training continues for up to another `patience` passes and the returned parameters change. TrainingProgress's docstring ('everything a run must carry across a machine dying') also leaves out this stop state. | confirmed |
| T-training-013 | `operators/training/loop.py` | Train times only the current call's loop and rewrites `<run>_manifest.json` on every resume. A relaunch that resumes an already finished stage, which Staged_Training does for every stage, replaces that stage's recorded training time with about a microsecond and sets resumed_from_step to the whole step count. Eight stage manifests no longer record how long their stages trained. The wall-clock is also missing from the checkpoint, so time spent before a power loss is never recorded. The results page gives the weight-tied-with-injection rung no wall-clock ('resumed across two power losses'), and its stage-0 and stage-1 manifests now read 8.9e-07 s and 2.9e-06 s. | confirmed |
| T-training-014 | `operators/training/sampling.py` | Spacing flat indices evenly aliases with the grid. On power-of-two cube grids the held-out points collapse onto one or two planes. The validation score that picks each stage's best parameters, and DeepONet's step-count candidate, is therefore measured on a biased slice: for the perovskite angle stratum it is a single plane through the atoms whose mean density is about 1.8x the field mean. Test_A_Fixed_Spread_Covers_A_Field_And_Never_Repeats checks only that the indices are distinct and in range, not coverage. | confirmed |
| T-training-015 | `operators/training/loader.py` | The fold-map loaders send every role except the literal 'evaluation' to the training folds and never refuse an unknown role. A caller that asks the perovskite or paired-field card for 'validation' or 'test' therefore gets the training set without warning, while the strain-atlas loader answers 'evaluation' with nothing. No current caller does this; the drivers carve their validation slices out of 'train'. | confirmed |
| T-training-016 | `operators/training/loop.py` | Any checkpoint whose parameter names match is accepted, even one written for a different configuration: a repaired member, a changed standardization or a different width. Staged_Training resumes every stage, so reusing a run name would silently continue the void run. Only renaming the repaired runs to _v2 prevented this. | confirmed |
| T-training-017 | `operators/codomain_attention/report.py` | Train_Completion_Member passes `lambda: ParameterSet(values=member.Parameter_Values())` as fresh_parameters, where `member = Fresh_Completion_Member(statistics, seed=seed)` is a newly initialized member. The fine-tune's pretrained weights enter only through `parameters = starting_parameters`. If a fine-tune's probe fails at the peak rate, Staged_Training's single retry therefore restarts from random initialization instead of the pretrained weights. drivers.py's Run_Fine_Tune and the K3 'pretrained_continued' arm (both `starting_parameters=progress.best_parameters`) would then silently train from scratch while still labeled as continued from the pretrain. | confirmed |

The number happens to match a genuine global fit to five decimals. | dropped (refuted) — blind-key K20 refuted: the canon never specified a cross-pair affine map; 'global' means spatially global, one slope and intercept per pair over the whole field, and the code says so |

## Phase A (the conductor's mechanical sweeps)

- **PA-01** (S4) `operators/training/staged.py`: Staged training returns the best parameters of the last stage only, not the best across stages; two reported flagship rungs are evaluated on parameters worse on validation than ones the run had kept.
- **PA-02** (S4) `/Pool/VASP_DATA/_derived/_training/nonlinear_manifold_decoder/manifold_stage0_manifest.json`: Zero-step resumes rewrote eight stage manifests with a wall clock of zero; the original training times survive only as checkpoint dates.
- **PA-03** (S4) `operators/training/loop.py`: Training manifests record no code commit, configuration or data identity, so no reported number can be tied to the code that produced it from the artifacts alone.
- **PA-04** (S2) `operator-results.md`: The results page states the gate ran 52,283 steps in two hours; it ran 33,170 training steps (plus a 200-step probe) in about 78 minutes, stopping early.
- **PA-05** (S5) `commit:a5cd89f`: Both runs trained 22,090 steps, not 33,650; the schedule was identical and the stopping point happened to coincide, but no run completed 33,650 steps.
- **PA-06** (S2) `operator-results.md`: The manifold decoder is compared with leave-one-level-out floors on the interior arm levels, but 80% of those levels were in its own training data; the page and report present the block as leave-one-level-out for all rows. The kill stands (the member lost with the advantage), but the comparison is not like for like.
- **PA-07** (S2) `operator-results.md`: The Galerkin gate's interpolation comparison scores the member in-sample on 24 of 27 levels against an out-of-sample floor, undisclosed. The kill stands (the member lost with the advantage).
- **PA-08** (S4) `operators/multiple_input_operator_network/cache.py`: The two-input network's fixed bases, reference density and potential standardization are fit on folds 1-4, so the validation fold that drives early stopping is inside the model's own fixed components; the test fold is untouched and the twin shares the density basis, so the member-against-twin verdict is unaffected.
